## Project Completion Targets / Planning Horizon

These figures are **estimates**, **planning targets**, and **subject to revision**. They are not guarantees, architecture invariants, or a mandatory stopping point.

- **Estimated total project size:** 220–260 chunks (planning estimate; subject to revision)
- **Central planning estimate:** ~240 chunks (planning estimate; subject to revision)
- **Current repository position:** Chunk 209 (published). Chunk 210 implemented/validated locally but unpublished.
- **Planning baseline established around Chunk 87:** ~133–173 chunks remaining; central estimate ~153 remaining (planning estimate; subject to revision)

`chunk-240` is **not** a mandatory stopping point. The project ends when required engineering goals are actually complete.

- **Project start:** 4 September 2026
- **Planning target for first serious complete end-to-end engineering v1:** approximately 7–21 October 2026 (planning target; subject to revision)
- **Central expectation:** ~4–6 additional weeks of intensive daily development from the 9 September planning point (planning estimate; subject to revision)

**Engineering/platform completion** (planning target: roughly first half / middle of October 2026) means the platform can work end-to-end with synthetic, public, or file-based inputs. That is distinct from **Armenia-specific live trading validation**, which requires authoritative real data and market validation and cannot honestly be dated until those sources are available. Future status wording may be: `Armenia-specific model calibration and live-market validation pending authoritative data access.` Engineering completion does not equal live-market validation.

**Engineering v1** is a planning definition only. It may legitimately include 13 agent boundaries/implementations at required maturity, full orchestration, adapters/ACL, databases, Regulatory RAG architecture, document indexing/search, ML training plus inference framework, synthetic/public-data experimentation, backtesting/simulation, portfolio/risk logic, trading strategy flow, billing/settlement, API surface, tests, observability foundation, failure handling, and production-oriented composition/runtime.

The first 80+ chunks established reusable patterns: ports, canonical DTOs, error contracts, async conventions, composition roots, provider adapters, settings patterns, lifespan patterns, architecture tests, and publication discipline. Some future agents/features may therefore implement faster. Velocity is still expected to vary and will slow in empirical areas such as ML experiments, real datasets, feature engineering, backtesting, market validation, live integrations, edge cases, and production hardening. Do not assume linear acceleration.

Lack of authoritative Armenian market data must not stop unrelated platform engineering. External sources remain replaceable behind the existing ACL:

```text
API / Excel / CSV / manual file / scraper / public dataset / synthetic dataset
        ↓
source-specific Adapter / ACL
        ↓
canonical internal model
        ↓
rest of platform
```

Source schemas must not leak into application, domain, or ML. Three planning data levels (not implemented by this chunk):

1. **Level 1 — Synthetic / fixture data.** Realistic synthetic series can validate ingestion, forecast pipelines, risk, trading, settlement, and end-to-end orchestration, including seasonality, hourly peaks, weather influence, noise, missing intervals, anomalies, and price volatility. Synthetic data proves that the engineering system works. It does **not** prove that the model performs well on the real Armenian market.
2. **Level 2 — Public / proxy datasets.** May validate ML engineering, backtesting infrastructure, and general forecasting behavior. They must not be presented as Armenia-specific model-quality evidence.
3. **Level 3 — File-first future integration.** Later authoritative data may arrive through messy Excel, CSV, API, manual files, or scraped sources and must enter via source-specific adapters/ACL. Unreadable/unmappable inputs follow existing diagnostic/DLQ policy.

## What this repository is

A reproducible Python 3.12 application skeleton with typed settings, a FastAPI factory, process health, canonical domain contracts, transport-neutral application errors, a standard API error envelope, correlation IDs, structured JSON logging, an application-facing structured ingestion boundary, a deterministic infrastructure-local schema field-resolution engine, a concrete Consumption CSV adapter, a concrete Consumption Excel `.xlsx` adapter, explicit Consumption MW/kW plus IANA timezone normalization, fail-closed Consumption duplicate detection, optional interval-grid alignment, per-consumer (`PdfTextExtractionAdapter`) and no OCR adapter, an application-owned document embedding port (`DocumentEmbeddingPort`, `DocumentChunkEmbedding`) structurally implemented by infrastructure The graph catches only `BaseExceptionGroup` at the Phase 2 step boundary and delegates that group plus the original `WorkflowState` to the injected `ParallelIngestionFailureRuntimeHandlingService`. The failure transition, the failure-policy decision service, the context builder, the failure-context resolution service, the failure-context preparation service, the initial terminal-fail failure policy, the failure-action executor, the prepared failure-handling composition, the ExceptionGroup extractor, the sanitized-fact classifier, the tuple-level classifier, the failure-fact selection port, the strict single-failure selector, the attempt-number source port, and the initial attempt-number source are not imported or invoked by LangGraph. Supported one-attributed-failure groups terminate at `ingestion`/`failed`. Multiple attributed failures and unattributed leaves remain fail-closed. Direct non-group exceptions still propagate. Retry-capable tracking, increment/reset semantics, and multi-failure selection still do not exist. Application also owns a typed Consumer Load Forecast model inference boundary (ConsumerLoadForecastModelPort, ConsumerLoadForecastModelRequest) that identifies the target consumer with canonical EntityId, reuses canonical ConsumptionRecord history (value_mw load/power in MW, not MWh) plus explicit UTC target_timestamps and returns tuple[LoadForecastPoint, ...]; empty history remains valid and non-empty history must belong to that consumer. Application-owned ConsumerLoadForecastAgent injects that port and returns the exact forecast tuple without numerical calculation; ML-owned `PreviousDayPersistenceConsumerLoadForecastModel` structurally implements that port as an unwired exact `T - 24h` MW baseline; ML-owned `build_previous_day_persistence_backtest_cases` builds chronological exact-pair evaluation cases; ML-owned `evaluate_previous_day_persistence_mae` scores those already-built cases into MW MAE with empty input failing closed; ML-owned `build_consumer_load_lag_24h_feature_rows` builds chronological exact 24h supervised feature rows (`ConsumerLoadLag24hFeatureRow`: `lag_24h_mw` feature, `target_value_mw` label); ML-owned `split_consumer_load_feature_rows_chronologically` partitions those already-built rows at an explicit UTC cutoff; ML-owned `fit_consumer_load_lag_24h_linear_regression` fits one-feature OLS `slope` and signed `intercept_mw` from already-built training rows without predicting or scoring; there is no LangGraph/API wiring of that agent, port, baseline, backtest builder, MAE evaluator, feature-row builder, splitter, or OLS fitter. Application also owns a typed DAM Price Forecast model inference boundary (DAMPriceForecastModelPort, DAMPriceForecastModelRequest) whose request now carries caller-supplied forecast_run_id and generated_at identity (Chunk 169) alongside explicit market identity, explicit currency, canonical MarketPriceRecord history, and explicit UTC target_timestamps; market and currency are identified independently of history, reuses canonical MarketPriceRecord history plus explicit UTC target_timestamps, and returns tuple[PriceForecastPoint, ...]; empty history remains valid and non-empty history must match that market and currency; there is no currency conversion. Application-owned DAMPriceForecastAgent injects that port and returns the exact forecast tuple without numerical calculation or currency conversion; there is no production ML adapter and no LangGraph/API wiring of that agent or port. Application-owned `ForecastingPlan` composes those two published request DTOs without executing either agent, choosing concurrency or Consumer → DAM ordering, or expanding `WorkflowState`. Application-owned `ForecastingSuccess` composes the two existing canonical forecast-point tuples without executing either agent, choosing concurrency or Consumer → DAM ordering, or expanding `WorkflowState`. Application-owned `ForecastingExecutionPort` is the async seam `execute(*, plan: ForecastingPlan) -> ForecastingSuccess` implemented by unwired `ParallelForecastingExecutionService` that concurrently executes Consumer Load and DAM agents with `asyncio.TaskGroup`. Application-owned `ForecastingWorkflowContextPort` resolves that plan from `WorkflowState` and records that success outside the snapshot; infrastructure `InMemoryForecastingWorkflowContext` is an unwired process-local local/dev structural implementation and is not durable production storage. Application-owned `ForecastingWorkflowStep` composes resolve → execute → record and returns the original `WorkflowState` unchanged; concurrent Consumer Load and DAM execution is owned by `ParallelForecastingExecutionService`. Application-owned `advance_after_forecasting` maps a valid `forecasting`/`running` snapshot to `risk_and_bid`/`running` without embedding forecast payloads; LangGraph node `forecasting_success_transition` applies it. It is **not** a running trading platform.

## Implemented artifacts

- Root living docs, `.cursorrules`, `.gitignore`, `.env.example`
- Python 3.12 project baseline (`.python-version`, `requires-python = ">=3.12,<3.13"`)
- uv dependency management with committed `uv.lock`
- Typed application settings (`AppSettings`)
- Typed PostgreSQL settings (`DatabaseSettings`) loaded separately from process health
- Typed Redis settings (`RedisSettings`) loaded separately from process health
- Typed Qdrant settings (`QdrantSettings`) loaded separately from process health
- Typed OpenAI settings (`OpenAISettings`) loaded separately from process health (`api_key: SecretStr`; `ENERGY_OPENAI_API_KEY`; no model fields)
- Typed Regulatory Intelligence runtime settings (`RegulatoryIntelligenceRuntimeSettings`) loaded separately from process health (`ENERGY_REGULATORY_*`; query-embedding model, constraint-inference model, Qdrant collection name, positive vector size; no secrets, clients, defaults, or `QdrantDocumentVectorConfig` construction)
- Typed document vector index runtime settings (`DocumentVectorIndexRuntimeSettings`) loaded separately from process health (`ENERGY_DOCUMENT_INDEX_*`; document-embedding model, Qdrant collection name, positive vector size; no secrets, clients, defaults, query/inference models, or `QdrantDocumentVectorConfig` construction; separate from `RegulatoryIntelligenceRuntimeSettings`)
- FastAPI application factory (`create_app`)
- `GET /api/v1/health` (process/application health only)
- `POST /api/v1/regulatory-intelligence/query` (production `create_app()` installs the published Regulatory query router; execution still depends on the lifespan-scoped service)
- `POST /api/v1/document-vector-index/index` (production `create_app()` installs the published Document Vector Index indexing router; request body is already-normalized chunk data only; execution still depends on the lifespan-scoped service; HTTP 204)
- Canonical Pydantic domain contracts (`energy_trading.domain.models`)
- Canonical UTC timestamp normalization (`UtcDateTime`)
- Typed MW / MWh constraints
- Decimal monetary and energy-price value objects (`MoneyAmount`, `EnergyPrice`)
- Regulatory, forecast, risk, trading, and settlement contracts
- Adapter diagnostics and DLQ metadata contract (`payload_reference` only)
- Transport-neutral application error hierarchy (`ApplicationError` and subclasses)
- Standardized API error envelope with centralized HTTP exception translation
- Sanitized validation errors (no raw `input`) and sanitized unexpected 500 handling
- Correlation ID middleware and `ContextVar` context
- Structured JSON logging and HTTP request completion logs
- Generic async structured-ingestion application port (`StructuredIngestionPort`)
- Immutable structured ingestion result (`StructuredIngestionResult`)
- Canonical diagnostics/DLQ metadata propagation on the ingestion envelope
- Application-owned DLQ sink port (`DeadLetterQueuePort`)
- Partial-success ingestion semantics (success, partial, complete normalization failure, valid empty source)
- Deterministic schema field resolution inside the infrastructure ACL (`DeterministicFieldResolver`)
- Unicode field-name normalization, exact alias matching, and stdlib fuzzy matching with confidence/ambiguity
- Schema-level missing-required-field and destination-collision reporting
- Shared Consumption field-profile and unit-safe fuzzy mapping policy (MW and kW profiles)
- Concrete `ConsumptionCsvAdapter` implementing `StructuredIngestionPort[ConsumptionRecord]`
- Stdlib CSV acquisition (`csv.reader`, UTF-8 including BOM) behind `asyncio.to_thread`
- Concrete `ConsumptionExcelAdapter` implementing `StructuredIngestionPort[ConsumptionRecord]`
- openpyxl `.xlsx` acquisition in read-only/data-only mode behind `asyncio.to_thread`
- Integration of Chunk 5 schema resolution with Consumption CSV and Excel headers
- Explicit Consumption MW/kW power normalization (`PowerUnit`) with deterministic kW→MW conversion
- Explicit IANA source-timezone normalization (`zoneinfo` + `tzdata`); aware timestamps keep their instant
- DST-ambiguous and nonexistent local clocks fail closed (no fold policy)
- Canonical `ConsumptionRecord` construction from CSV and Excel rows (canonical output remains MW + UTC)
- Fail-closed Consumption duplicate detection on `(consumer_id, canonical UTC timestamp)` **within one `ingest()` batch**
- Optional explicit interval-grid alignment (`IntervalGrid`); default is disabled
- Per-consumer internal gap detection on an explicit `IntervalGrid` cadence
- Compact contiguous gap reporting (`missing_count` plus first/last missing timestamps, infrastructure-only)
- CSV/XLSX sanitized gap diagnostics (`consumption_missing_interval_gap`) with no fabricated DLQ for missing source rows
- Partial success plus canonical DLQ metadata (adapters do not persist it)
- Filesystem-backed `FilesystemDeadLetterQueue` implementing `DeadLetterQueuePort` (canonical metadata JSON only; single-record idempotent enqueue by `record_id`)
- Application-owned unstructured document extraction port (`DocumentExtractionPort`)
- Immutable `ExtractedDocumentChunk` and `DocumentExtractionResult` application DTOs (normalized text only; no embeddings, paths, or raw bytes)
- Concrete infrastructure `PdfTextExtractionAdapter` structurally implementing that port for constructor-configured local text-layer PDFs (`pypdf`, page-based chunks, `asyncio.to_thread`); no OCR; unwired from `create_app()`, Regulatory runtime, document-index runtime, LangGraph, OpenAI, and Qdrant
- Application-owned document embedding port (`DocumentEmbeddingPort`)
- Immutable `DocumentChunkEmbedding` application DTO (opaque document/chunk identity plus a finite float vector; no provider/model/distance fields)
- Application-owned document query-text embedding port (`DocumentQueryEmbeddingPort`)
- Immutable `DocumentQueryEmbedding` application DTO (finite float vector only; no query text, provider, model, identity, or search fields)
- Application-owned `DocumentVectorSearchQueryPreparationService` (injects `DocumentQueryEmbeddingPort`; keyword-only `async prepare(*, query_text, limit) -> DocumentVectorSearchQuery`; query text forwarded unchanged; existing query-contract limit validation preserved; no search invocation; not injected into `RegulatoryIntelligenceAgent`; graph and API remain unwired)
- Query-preparation unit and architecture-boundary tests (structural embedding fake; no live provider, search, or graph/API wiring)
- Application-owned `DocumentVectorIndexEntryPreparationService` (injects `DocumentEmbeddingPort`; keyword-only `async prepare(*, chunks) -> tuple[DocumentVectorIndexEntry, ...]`; one `embed(...)` call; input order and canonical `(document_id, chunk_id)` identity preserved; empty input returns `()`; count/identity mismatch fails closed as sanitized `DependencyUnavailableError`; no `DocumentVectorIndexPort.index`; not wired to OpenAI, Qdrant, `create_app()`, Regulatory runtime, LangGraph, or extraction)
- Document vector index-entry preparation unit and architecture-boundary tests (structural embedding fake; no live provider, index invocation, or graph/API wiring)
- Application-owned `DocumentVectorIndexExecutionService` (injects `DocumentVectorIndexEntryPreparationService` and `DocumentVectorIndexPort`; keyword-only `async execute(*, chunks) -> None`; one `prepare(...)` then one `index(...)`; exact chunks and prepared entries forwarded unchanged; empty input prepares then indexes `()`; no `embed(...)` and no entry construction; not wired to OpenAI, Qdrant, `create_app()`, Regulatory runtime, LangGraph, or extraction)
- Document vector index execution unit and architecture-boundary tests (preparation and index doubles; no live provider, Qdrant, or graph/API wiring)
- Application-owned `DocumentExtractionIndexExecutionService` (injects `DocumentExtractionPort` and `DocumentVectorIndexExecutionService`; `async execute() -> DocumentExtractionResult`; one `extract()`; indexes only when `result.chunks` is non-empty by forwarding the exact chunk tuple; empty chunks skip indexing; returns the original extraction result unchanged; extraction and indexing exceptions propagate unchanged; not wired to `create_app()`, Regulatory runtime, document-index runtime, LangGraph, OpenAI, Qdrant, or `PdfTextExtractionAdapter`)
- Document extraction-to-index execution unit and architecture-boundary tests (structural extraction and index-execution doubles; no live PDF, OpenAI, Qdrant, filesystem, or graph/API wiring)
- API-owned `build_pdf_document_extraction_index_execution` (keyword-only synchronous builder over local `Path`, `document_id`, `source_name`, and an already-constructed `DocumentVectorIndexExecutionService`; constructs exactly one `PdfTextExtractionAdapter` and one `DocumentExtractionIndexExecutionService`; preserves index-service identity; construction performs no extract/execute/file I/O, settings load, or provider construction; not wired to `create_app()`, production lifespan, document-index HTTP, Regulatory runtime, or LangGraph)
- PDF extraction-to-index composition unit and architecture-boundary tests (nonexistent PDF path; structural embedding/index doubles; no live PDF parse, OpenAI, Qdrant, filesystem availability check, or graph/API wiring)

- API-owned `execute_loaded_pdf_document_extraction_index` (keyword-only async function over local `Path`, `document_id`, `source_name`, and the existing `env_file` contract; enters Chunk 103 once; awaits `execute()` exactly once; returns the original `DocumentExtractionResult` identity; runtime-entry and execution failures propagate unchanged with inner teardown; not wired to `create_app()`, production lifespan, HTTP, startup, background, scheduler, Regulatory runtime, or LangGraph)
- One-shot loaded PDF extraction-to-index execution unit and architecture-boundary tests (patched Chunk 103 runtime; exact path/identity/`env_file` forwarding; exact result identity; runtime-entry failure skips execute; execute failure still tears down; no nested extract/index/PDF-adapter/provider calls)

- API-owned `build_document_vector_index_lifespan` (synchronous keyword-only factory; reuses the existing `env_file` contract; returns a FastAPI-compatible lifespan callback that enters/exits Chunk 91 around application lifespan; lazy construction; the exact yielded `DocumentVectorIndexExecutionService` is stored on `app.state.document_vector_index_execution_service` only while the lifespan is active and is removed before Chunk 91 teardown; not imported or called by production `create_app()`; entered only through `build_production_lifespan`; no HTTP route, provider SDK, settings-loader, execute/embed/index, or query-execution calls)
- API-owned `get_document_vector_index_execution_service` (synchronous request accessor in `api/dependencies`; reads exact `request.app.state.document_vector_index_execution_service`; returns that exact `DocumentVectorIndexExecutionService` identity; missing or wrong-type state fails closed as existing `DependencyUnavailableError` with sanitized message `Document Vector Index service is unavailable.`; no lifecycle ownership, no `.execute`; used by the Chunk 98 indexing router through `Depends`; not invoked by `create_app()`; production `create_app()` installs that router under the existing API prefix)
- API-owned HTTP request transport DTOs `DocumentVectorIndexChunkRequest` (`document_id`, `chunk_id`, `text`, `ordinal`, optional `page_number`) and `DocumentVectorIndexRequest` (`chunks` tuple of those chunk DTOs); frozen Pydantic models with `extra="forbid"`; they project already-normalized extracted-chunk fields only; no file/upload/path/URL/provider/vector fields; no response DTO; used by the Chunk 98 indexing router
- API-owned Document Vector Index HTTP indexing router (`api/routers/document_vector_index.py`; POST `/document-vector-index/index`; accepts the published request DTO; resolves the published service through `Depends(get_document_vector_index_execution_service)`; explicitly projects each chunk into `ExtractedDocumentChunk`; awaits `execute(chunks=...)` once; returns HTTP 204 with no body; no handler-local exception translation; production `create_app()` installs it once with `resolved_settings.api_prefix` so `POST /api/v1/document-vector-index/index` is reachable; request body remains already-normalized chunks only)
- API-owned `build_production_lifespan` (synchronous keyword-only factory; forwards the existing `env_file` unchanged to `build_regulatory_intelligence_lifespan` and `build_document_vector_index_lifespan`; nests Regulatory as the outer FastAPI lifespan and Document Vector Index as the inner lifespan; lazy construction; yields `None`; no `app.state` assignment, shared-client refactor, or generic lifespan framework; installed by production `create_app()` as the default FastAPI lifespan)

actual regulatory RAG runtime; verified Armenian DAM rule extraction; full Regulatory graph continuation and API graph composition; `create_app()` wiring of `build_regulatory_intelligence_query_execution`, `build_regulatory_intelligence_provider_runtime`, `build_regulatory_intelligence_configured_runtime`, `managed_regulatory_intelligence_runtime`, or `loaded_regulatory_intelligence_runtime`; LangGraph/API wiring of `RegulatoryIntelligenceQueryExecutionService`; Pricing & Sales Agent. Direct non-group Phase 2 exceptions still propagate from the graph. Supported one-attributed-failure groups now terminate at `INGESTION`/`FAILED` through LangGraph. Multiple attributed failures still fail closed. The graph factory does not auto-wire the strict selector, initial attempt source, initial terminal-fail policy, or lower-level failure internals. Failure handling is not complete. Parallel Phase 2 join is not complete. Regulatory RAG is not operational.

Current Phase 2 failure pipeline:

```text
Phase 2 ExceptionGroup
→ runtime handling service
→ failure-context preparation
→ extraction
→ tuple classification
→ context-resolution service
→ strict single selector
   exactly one fact → selected fact
   zero or multiple facts → fail closed
→ initial attempt-number source (`1`)
→ context builder
→ FailurePolicyContext
→ existing failure handling
→ initial failure policy
→ FailureAction.FAIL
→ existing terminal action
→ INGESTION / FAILED
→ LangGraph routes ingestion/failed to END
```

Six concrete application agents exist. The five Phase 2 ingestion agents remain provider-neutral. LangGraph does not import them; they are invoked only if a caller composes the execution port behind `ParallelIngestionWorkflowStep`. They remain unwired to API, providers, persistence, and n8n. Regulatory Intelligence is also provider-neutral at the application layer and unwired from LangGraph: it composes existing document-vector retrieval with an injected inference port. Infrastructure now has an OpenAI inference adapter behind that port, but the application agent does not import OpenAI. Application can compose query text + limit → `DocumentVectorSearchQueryPreparationService` → `RegulatoryIntelligenceRequest` → `RegulatoryIntelligenceAgent` → `RegulatoryIntelligenceResult` through `RegulatoryIntelligenceQueryExecutionService`. The API-owned `build_regulatory_intelligence_query_execution` constructs that stack from three injected application ports. The API-owned `build_regulatory_intelligence_provider_runtime` constructs `OpenAIDocumentQueryEmbeddingAdapter`, `QdrantDocumentVectorSearch`, and `OpenAIRegulatoryConstraintInferenceAdapter` from injected clients, existing Qdrant vector config, and two explicit model strings, then delegates to that provider-neutral builder. Configured `build_regulatory_intelligence_configured_runtime` accepts the same injected clients plus already-constructed `RegulatoryIntelligenceRuntimeSettings`, maps collection name and vector size into one `QdrantDocumentVectorConfig`, forwards the two model fields, and delegates to that provider-aware builder. Managed `managed_regulatory_intelligence_runtime` accepts already-loaded `OpenAISettings`, `QdrantSettings`, and `RegulatoryIntelligenceRuntimeSettings`, constructs clients through existing factories, delegates to that configured builder, yields the query-execution service, and closes both clients in LIFO order. Typed `RegulatoryIntelligenceRuntimeSettings` now holds those two model identifiers plus Regulatory Qdrant collection name and vector size under `ENERGY_REGULATORY_*`, separately from `OpenAISettings` and `QdrantSettings`. That is typed configuration plus resource lifetime, not production RAG readiness: OpenAI/Qdrant client **construction** exists through typed settings plus lazy factories; Chunk 74 can own those clients for the duration of an `async with`; Chunk 75 can load the three typed settings through existing loaders and delegate to that managed runtime. Chunk 76 can own FastAPI-compatible entry/exit of that loaded runtime without exposing the service to HTTP handlers or LangGraph. Chunk 77 first installed that lifespan into production `create_app()` by default; Chunk 94 now enters it through `build_production_lifespan` while preserving the no-op test lifespan seam. Chunk 81 adds a dedicated HTTP query router that uses `Depends` and invokes the lifespan-scoped service. Chunk 82 installs that published router in production `create_app()` under the existing API prefix so `POST /api/v1/regulatory-intelligence/query` is reachable. Construction remains lazy and tests may inject a no-op lifespan. Execution still depends on the lifespan-scoped service. Chunk 99 installs the published Document Vector Index indexing router under the same API prefix so `POST /api/v1/document-vector-index/index` is reachable for already-normalized chunks only. Qdrant collection provisioning, automatic corpus ingestion, OCR, production wiring of `DocumentExtractionIndexExecutionService`, and LangGraph Phase 1 wiring remain absent. The Chunk 71 builder still requires caller-supplied clients/config values and does not load these settings. The Chunk 73 builder still requires caller-supplied clients plus an already-constructed settings object and does not load environment values. The Chunk 74 managed runtime still requires already-loaded settings objects and does not load environment values. Do not claim the trading workflow runs end-to-end.

## Known issues

- Armenian DAM official products, gate times, bid envelope, currency, and settlement math are **unverified** and must not be hardcoded.
- Concrete external API providers are **not** selected.
- WSL2 RAM cap vs future Compose services is an operational risk (see `ARCHITECTURE.md`).
- TimescaleDB is pinned to `timescale/timescaledb:2.29.2-pg17` for local development; it is on-demand, not always running.
- Redis is pinned to `redis:8.2.9-alpine` for local development; it is on-demand, not always running.
- Qdrant is pinned to `qdrant/qdrant:v1.19.1` for local development; it is on-demand, not always running. Local HTTP plus API key is not the production security model.
- n8n is pinned to `n8nio/n8n:2.37.10` for local development; it is on-demand, not always running. Local HTTP plus a deployment encryption key is not the production security model.

## Architectural constraints (in force)

- Clean Architecture: `domain` ← `application` ← `api` / composition root; `infrastructure` and `ml` implement application ports and use domain contracts
- Consumer Load Forecast numerical inference crosses application through `ConsumerLoadForecastModelPort` only (`ConsumerLoadForecastModelRequest` → `tuple[LoadForecastPoint, ...]`). The request requires caller-supplied `forecast_run_id` and `generated_at` plus the target consumer with canonical `EntityId` independently of history. Those identity fields are not generated by the request DTO, the agent, or a clock. Historical observations reuse `ConsumptionRecord.value_mw` (MW load/power, not MWh) and, when present, must belong to that same consumer; empty history remains structurally valid. An unwired ML-owned `PreviousDayPersistenceConsumerLoadForecastModel` structurally implements that port as exact `T - 24h` MW copy with fail-closed missing/duplicate lag and caller-supplied identity passthrough. An unwired ML-owned `build_consumer_load_lag_24h_feature_rows` builder derives chronological exact 24h supervised feature rows from canonical `ConsumptionRecord` history without depending on inference, backtest, or MAE artifacts. An unwired ML-owned `split_consumer_load_feature_rows_chronologically` splitter partitions those already-built rows at an explicit UTC cutoff (`training_rows` where `target_timestamp < cutoff`, `evaluation_rows` where `target_timestamp >= cutoff`) without depending on inference, backtest, MAE, or history rebuilding. An unwired ML-owned `fit_consumer_load_lag_24h_linear_regression` fitter returns OLS `slope` and signed `intercept_mw` from already-built training rows without depending on the split type, prediction, or metrics. Future trained `energy_trading.ml` adapters may use vendor arrays internally but must not leak DataFrames, ndarrays, or model files across that boundary. The baseline, feature-row builder, chronological splitter, and OLS fitter are not wired into agents, API, LangGraph, or `ForecastingExecutionPort`.
- Mandatory Anti-Corruption Layer
- Canonical Pydantic contracts (implemented)
- Application-facing structured ingestion receives canonical models only
- Raw external field names stay inside infrastructure schema mapping; they do not cross Chunk 4 application ports
- Consumption CSV/Excel may convert explicitly configured kW to MW and localize naive timestamps with an explicit IANA zone; units and timezones are never inferred; canonical output remains MW + UTC
- Consumption duplicate groups fail closed inside one `ingest()` batch; interval cadence is validated only against an explicit adapter `IntervalGrid`
- Missing intervals are reported only inside an observed per-consumer span; they do not fabricate DLQ records or synthetic observations
- Filesystem DLQ persistence stores canonical `DLQRecord` metadata only, is not wired to adapters or `create_app()`, and does not replace PostgreSQL as the planned system of record
- Unstructured document extraction is an application port returning normalized text chunks; it is not a `RegulatoryConstraint`. Infrastructure `PdfTextExtractionAdapter` structurally implements that port for local text-layer PDFs only; OCR remains absent; the adapter is unwired from `create_app()`, production lifespan, document-index HTTP, Regulatory runtime, and LangGraph
- Document extraction-to-index execution is an application-owned `DocumentExtractionIndexExecutionService` over `DocumentExtractionPort` and `DocumentVectorIndexExecutionService`; `execute()` extracts once, indexes only non-empty `result.chunks`, and returns the original `DocumentExtractionResult` identity; empty extraction does not call indexing; it does not reconstruct results, synthesize diagnostics, or wire `create_app()` / Regulatory runtime / document-index runtime / LangGraph / OpenAI / Qdrant
- PDF extraction-to-index composition is an API-owned synchronous `build_pdf_document_extraction_index_execution` over a local `Path`, `document_id`, `source_name`, and an already-constructed `DocumentVectorIndexExecutionService`; it constructs `PdfTextExtractionAdapter` and `DocumentExtractionIndexExecutionService` without extracting, indexing, inspecting the filesystem, loading settings, or constructing OpenAI/Qdrant clients; application does not import this builder; `create_app()`, production lifespan, document-index HTTP, Regulatory runtime, and LangGraph remain unwired; this is not automatic corpus ingestion
- Settings-loaded PDF extraction-to-index composition is an API-owned async `loaded_pdf_document_extraction_index_runtime` over a local `Path`, `document_id`, `source_name`, and the existing `env_file` contract; it enters `loaded_document_vector_index_runtime` once, forwards the yielded `DocumentVectorIndexExecutionService` plus path/identities to Chunk 102, and yields that `DocumentExtractionIndexExecutionService` unchanged; it does not extract, index, inspect the filesystem, own provider clients, or load settings itself; application does not import this runtime; `create_app()`, production lifespan, document-index HTTP, Regulatory runtime, and LangGraph remain unwired; this is not automatic corpus ingestion
- One-shot loaded PDF extraction-to-index execution is an API-owned async `execute_loaded_pdf_document_extraction_index` over a local `Path`, `document_id`, `source_name`, and the existing `env_file` contract; it enters Chunk 103 once, awaits `execute()` exactly once, and returns the original `DocumentExtractionResult` identity; it does not call nested extraction or index ports, reconstruct results, or wire `create_app()` / production lifespan / HTTP / startup / background / scheduler / LangGraph; this is explicit local text-layer PDF → extraction → embedding → vector-index execution when called, not automatic corpus ingestion
- Document embedding is an application-owned `DocumentEmbeddingPort` over already-normalized `ExtractedDocumentChunk` values; it is not Qdrant and not a domain contract. Infrastructure `OpenAIDocumentEmbeddingAdapter` structurally implements that port with an injected `AsyncOpenAI` client and explicit model; it does not construct the client, load settings, or wire `create_app()` / Regulatory runtime / LangGraph / extraction / Qdrant indexing
- Document vector index-entry preparation is an application-owned `DocumentVectorIndexEntryPreparationService` over that embedding port; keyword-only `prepare(*, chunks)` returns existing `DocumentVectorIndexEntry` values in input order; empty input returns `()`; count/identity mismatch fails closed; it does not call `DocumentVectorIndexPort.index()`, import OpenAI, or wire `create_app()` / Regulatory runtime / LangGraph / extraction / Qdrant indexing
- Document vector index execution is an application-owned `DocumentVectorIndexExecutionService` over that preparation service plus `DocumentVectorIndexPort`; keyword-only `execute(*, chunks)` prepares once then indexes once and returns `None`; empty input prepares then indexes `()`; it does not embed, construct entries, import OpenAI/Qdrant, or wire `create_app()` / Regulatory runtime / LangGraph / extraction
- Document vector index execution composition is an API-owned synchronous `build_document_vector_index_execution` over `DocumentEmbeddingPort` and `DocumentVectorIndexPort`; it constructs index-entry preparation and index execution without provider construction, settings/environment access, I/O, or runtime method calls; application does not import this builder; `create_app()` does not invoke it; it does not construct OpenAI or Qdrant adapters
- Document vector index provider-aware composition is an API-owned synchronous `build_document_vector_index_provider_runtime` over already-created `AsyncOpenAI`, `AsyncQdrantClient`, existing `QdrantDocumentVectorConfig`, and an explicit document-embedding model string; it constructs `OpenAIDocumentEmbeddingAdapter` and `QdrantDocumentVectorIndex` then delegates once to the Chunk 86 builder; it is offline, synchronous, and inert; it remains unwired from `create_app()`, Regulatory runtime lifecycle, LangGraph, and document extraction
- Document vector index configured composition is an API-owned synchronous `build_document_vector_index_configured_runtime` over already-created `AsyncOpenAI`, `AsyncQdrantClient`, and already-constructed `DocumentVectorIndexRuntimeSettings`; it constructs one `QdrantDocumentVectorConfig` from collection name and vector size, forwards the document-embedding model, and delegates once to the Chunk 87 builder; it is offline, synchronous, and inert; it remains unwired from `create_app()`, FastAPI lifespan, LangGraph, and document extraction; it does not execute indexing, own clients, or load settings
- Document vector index managed composition is an API-owned async `managed_document_vector_index_runtime` over already-loaded `OpenAISettings`, `QdrantSettings`, `DocumentVectorIndexRuntimeSettings`, and `QdrantDocumentVectorDistanceSettings`; it constructs clients through existing factories, registers async `close()` immediately, awaits Chunk 110 collection ensure once on that same managed Qdrant client, then delegates once to the Chunk 89 builder, yields that `DocumentVectorIndexExecutionService` unchanged, and closes clients in LIFO order (Qdrant, then OpenAI); collection-ensure failure propagates unchanged and still closes both clients; it does not load environment values, import provider SDKs, construct a second Qdrant client, execute indexing, or call create/verify/mapping directly
- Document vector index settings-loaded composition is an API-owned async `loaded_document_vector_index_runtime` with the existing `env_file` loader contract; it calls `load_openai_settings`, `load_qdrant_settings`, `load_document_vector_index_runtime_settings`, and `load_qdrant_document_vector_distance_settings` exactly once each, then delegates once to Chunk 90 and yields that `DocumentVectorIndexExecutionService` unchanged; it does not construct clients, import provider SDKs, call collection ensure, execute indexing, or wire FastAPI/`create_app()` / LangGraph
- Automatic production Document Vector Index collection provisioning exists indirectly through production lifespan → document-index lifespan → loaded runtime → managed runtime. It is not a second provisioning client. Regulatory collection automatic provisioning, shared Regulatory/index collection identity, collection migration, recreate/update/delete, race retry/locking, aliases, payload indexes, and schema versioning remain absent. Not all Qdrant collections are automatically provisioned.
- Document vector index FastAPI lifespan composition is an API-owned synchronous `build_document_vector_index_lifespan` that reuses the existing `env_file` contract and returns a FastAPI-compatible lifespan callback; constructing the callback is lazy; entering it enters Chunk 91 and exiting it tears Chunk 91 down; the exact yielded `DocumentVectorIndexExecutionService` is stored on `app.state.document_vector_index_execution_service` only while the lifespan is active and is removed before Chunk 91 teardown; production `create_app()` does not import or call this factory; Chunk 94 enters it only through `build_production_lifespan`; it does not execute indexing
- Document vector index request-time access is an API-owned synchronous `get_document_vector_index_execution_service` that reads `request.app.state.document_vector_index_execution_service` and returns that exact `DocumentVectorIndexExecutionService` identity; missing or wrong-type state fails closed as existing `DependencyUnavailableError`; the accessor does not assign or delete application state, wrap the service, or invoke `.execute(...)`; production `create_app()` does not wrap it in `Depends(...)`; the Chunk 98 indexing router uses `Depends` on that accessor and is installed by `create_app()` under the existing API prefix
- Document vector index HTTP request transport is API-owned: frozen `DocumentVectorIndexChunkRequest` and `DocumentVectorIndexRequest` in `api/schemas` carry already-normalized extracted-chunk fields only; unknown fields are forbidden; they are not `ExtractedDocumentChunk`, not a raw upload/acquisition surface, and not a provider/vector contract; there is no response DTO
- Document vector index HTTP indexing is an API-owned router: `POST /document-vector-index/index` accepts the published request DTO, explicitly projects `ExtractedDocumentChunk` values, awaits `DocumentVectorIndexExecutionService.execute(*, chunks=...)` once, and returns 204 No Content; production `create_app()` includes it once with `resolved_settings.api_prefix` so `POST /api/v1/document-vector-index/index` is reachable; the request body remains already-normalized chunks only
- Production FastAPI lifespan composition is an API-owned synchronous `build_production_lifespan` that forwards the existing `env_file` unchanged to the two published child lifespan factories and nests Regulatory as the outer context and Document Vector Index as the inner context; constructing the callback is lazy; it yields `None` and does not write `app.state`; production `create_app()` defaults to this composite; it does not execute indexing or share provider clients
- Document vector index runtime configuration is shared typed settings (`DocumentVectorIndexRuntimeSettings`): required document-embedding model, Qdrant collection name, and strictly positive vector size under `ENERGY_DOCUMENT_INDEX_*`. It is separate from `AppSettings`, `OpenAISettings`, `QdrantSettings`, and `RegulatoryIntelligenceRuntimeSettings`. It holds no API keys, clients, adapters, query/inference models, prompts, or retry/lifecycle fields, and it does not construct `QdrantDocumentVectorConfig`. `create_app()`, the Chunk 87 builder, the Chunk 89 builder, and the Chunk 90 manager do not load it. Chunk 91 loads it through the published loader.
- Query-text embedding is an application-owned `DocumentQueryEmbeddingPort` over already-normalized query text returning `DocumentQueryEmbedding`; it is not document-chunk embedding, not vector search, not Qdrant, and not a domain contract. Infrastructure `OpenAIDocumentQueryEmbeddingAdapter` structurally implements that port with an injected `AsyncOpenAI` client and explicit model; it does not construct the client
- Query-search-query preparation is an application-owned `DocumentVectorSearchQueryPreparationService` over that port plus an explicit caller-supplied `limit`, returning existing `DocumentVectorSearchQuery`; it does not search, rewrite query text, default/clamp limits, or inject into `RegulatoryIntelligenceAgent`; OpenAI adapter injection and model runtime selection remain deferred from this service
- Regulatory query execution is an application-owned `RegulatoryIntelligenceQueryExecutionService` over that preparation service plus the unchanged `RegulatoryIntelligenceAgent`; keyword-only `execute(*, query_text, limit)` constructs existing `RegulatoryIntelligenceRequest` and returns existing `RegulatoryIntelligenceResult` unchanged; it does not embed, search, infer, rewrite query text, or default/clamp limits; graph and `create_app()` remain unwired; OpenAI adapter injection and model runtime selection remain deferred from this service
- Regulatory runtime composition is an API-owned synchronous `build_regulatory_intelligence_query_execution` over the three published application ports; it constructs query preparation, the agent, and query execution without provider construction, settings/environment access, I/O, or runtime method calls; application does not import this builder; `create_app()` does not invoke it; it does not construct `AsyncOpenAI`, `OpenAIDocumentQueryEmbeddingAdapter`, or `OpenAIRegulatoryConstraintInferenceAdapter`
- Regulatory constraint inference is an application-owned `RegulatoryConstraintInferencePort` over already-normalized `ExtractedDocumentChunk` values returning canonical `RegulatoryConstraint` tuples. Infrastructure `OpenAIRegulatoryConstraintInferenceAdapter` structurally implements that port with an injected `AsyncOpenAI` client and explicit model; it uses Responses structured output, checks evidence chunk IDs against the supplied chunks, and converts private candidates into canonical `RegulatoryConstraint`. It does not construct the client, hardcode Armenian DAM rules, or wire `create_app()` / LangGraph. OpenAI SDK imports remain confined to the four approved infrastructure modules plus the two exact Regulatory API provider-composition modules (`api/composition/regulatory_intelligence_runtime.py` and `api/composition/regulatory_intelligence_configured_runtime.py`) and the two exact document-index provider-composition modules (`api/composition/document_vector_index_runtime.py` and `api/composition/document_vector_index_configured_runtime.py`); HTTP routes/`create_app()` remain provider-SDK-free
- OpenAI connectivity is infrastructure-only: typed `OpenAISettings` (`api_key: SecretStr`, `ENERGY_OPENAI_API_KEY`) plus lazy `create_openai_client()` returning `AsyncOpenAI` with `max_retries=0`; the settings module is SDK-free and separate from `AppSettings`; construction performs no network request; there is no global client and no model selection in settings; Chunk 74's managed runtime and Chunk 90's managed document-index runtime may call this factory from already-loaded settings, Chunk 75 and Chunk 91 may load those settings through `load_openai_settings`, but `create_app()` remains unwired
- Regulatory Intelligence runtime configuration is shared typed settings (`RegulatoryIntelligenceRuntimeSettings`): required query-embedding model, constraint-inference model, Qdrant collection name, and strictly positive vector size under `ENERGY_REGULATORY_*`. It is separate from `AppSettings`, `OpenAISettings`, and `QdrantSettings`. It holds no API keys, clients, adapters, prompts, or retry/lifecycle fields, and it does not construct `QdrantDocumentVectorConfig`. `create_app()` and the Chunk 71 builder do not load it. Chunk 73 and Chunk 74 receive an already-constructed instance and do not call the loader. Chunk 75 loads it through the published loader. Chunk 112 receives an already-constructed instance for verify-existing collection readiness and does not call the loader.
- Document vector indexing is an application-owned `DocumentVectorIndexPort` pairing a normalized chunk with its matching embedding; logical identity is `(document_id, chunk_id)`; exact retries are idempotent; conflicts fail closed; there is no generic `VectorStore`; infrastructure `QdrantDocumentVectorIndex` is the concrete writer
- Document vector retrieval is an application-owned `DocumentVectorSearchPort`; input is an already-embedded finite query vector plus a positive `limit`; output is ranked `ExtractedDocumentChunk` values with unique `(document_id, chunk_id)` identities; zero matches are valid; backend scores, collection names, filters, and query text do not cross the port; indexing and search remain separate; there is no generic `VectorStore`; infrastructure `QdrantDocumentVectorSearch` is the concrete reader
- Qdrant connectivity is infrastructure-only except the two exact Regulatory API provider-composition modules (`api/composition/regulatory_intelligence_runtime.py` and `api/composition/regulatory_intelligence_configured_runtime.py`), the two exact document-index provider-composition modules (`api/composition/document_vector_index_runtime.py` and `api/composition/document_vector_index_configured_runtime.py`), and the document-vector distance mapper (`api/composition/qdrant_document_vector_distance.py`), the configured document-index collection-ensure composition (`api/composition/document_vector_index_collection_ensure.py`), and the configured Regulatory collection-readiness composition (`api/composition/regulatory_intelligence_collection_readiness.py`): typed `QdrantSettings` plus lazy `create_qdrant_client()` remain unused by `build_regulatory_intelligence_provider_runtime`, `build_regulatory_intelligence_configured_runtime`, `build_document_vector_index_provider_runtime`, `build_document_vector_index_configured_runtime`, and `ensure_configured_document_vector_index_collection_ready`; Chunk 74's managed runtime may call that factory from already-loaded `QdrantSettings` without importing the Qdrant SDK; Chunk 90's managed document-index runtime may call that factory from already-loaded `QdrantSettings` without importing the Qdrant SDK; - Consumption persistence identity is `(consumer_id, timestamp)`; exact retries are idempotent; differing values conflict; adapters still do not query PostgreSQL
- Cache is an application-owned `CachePort[TValue]`; infrastructure `RedisCache` is TTL-bound and ephemeral; Compose `redis` profile is optional and on-demand; no API/orchestration wiring, no locks

- Agent invocation is application-owned: `AgentName` plus structurally typed `AgentPort[TRequest, TResult]`; only `name` and async `run(request)` are shared; no registry, retry, or fallback. - Workflow snapshot is application-owned: frozen `WorkflowState` with `WorkflowPhase` and `WorkflowStatus`; LangGraph consumes it as `state_schema` and does not redefine it; the snapshot has no transition methods or persistence, and no phase-specific canonical output slots
- Successful Phase 2 control-state transition is application-owned: `advance_after_parallel_ingestion(state)` is valid only for `ingestion`/`running`, returns a new seven-field snapshot at `forecasting`/`running`, preserves identity/date/diagnostics, and fails closed with `InvalidRequestError` otherwise. It is not a generic state machine and is not a method on `WorkflowState`. LangGraph node `parallel_ingestion_success_transition` delegates to this function and does not reimplement it
- Successful Phase 3 control-state transition is application-owned: `advance_after_forecasting(state)` is valid only for `forecasting`/`running`, returns a new seven-field snapshot at `risk_and_bid`/`running`, preserves identity/date/diagnostics, and fails closed with `InvalidRequestError` otherwise. It is not a generic state machine and is not a method on `WorkflowState`. LangGraph node `forecasting_success_transition` delegates to this function and does not reimplement it
- Terminal Phase 3 failure transition is application-owned: `fail_after_forecasting(state)` is valid only for `forecasting`/`running`, returns a new seven-field snapshot at `forecasting`/`failed`, preserves identity/date/diagnostics unchanged, and fails closed with `InvalidRequestError` otherwise. It does not append diagnostics, map exceptions, consult `FailurePolicyPort`, or imply retry/fallback/degraded continuation. It is not a generic state machine and is not a method on `WorkflowState`. LangGraph, the executor, and the workflow step do not call it
- Terminal Phase 2 failure transition is application-owned: `fail_parallel_ingestion(state)` is valid only for `ingestion`/`running`, returns a new seven-field snapshot at `ingestion`/`failed`, preserves identity/date/diagnostics unchanged, and fails closed with `InvalidRequestError` otherwise. It does not append diagnostics, map exceptions, consult `FailurePolicyPort`, or imply retry/fallback/degraded continuation. It is not a generic state machine and is not a method on `WorkflowState`. LangGraph does not call it directly; the injected runtime failure handler applies it through existing handling on the supported `BaseExceptionGroup` path.
- LangGraph is application-owned: `build_workflow_graph(*, regulatory_intelligence_node: RegulatoryIntelligenceWorkflowNodeAdapter, parallel_ingestion_step: ParallelIngestionWorkflowStep, parallel_ingestion_failure_runtime_handler: ParallelIngestionFailureRuntimeHandlingService, forecasting_step: ForecastingWorkflowStep, forecasting_failure_runtime_handler: ForecastingFailureRuntimeHandlingService)` compiles `START → workflow_entry`, then routes contract/running to `regulatory_intelligence` → `END`, ingestion/running to `parallel_ingestion`, and forecasting/running to `forecasting`; `workflow_entry` is async and no-op; `regulatory_intelligence` awaits the injected Chunk 116 adapter exactly once and remains terminal; `parallel_ingestion` awaits the injected step, catches only `BaseExceptionGroup`, and delegates that group plus the original state to the injected runtime handler; the Phase-2 success-transition node applies `advance_after_parallel_ingestion`; `forecasting` awaits the injected `ForecastingWorkflowStep` exactly once, catches only `BaseExceptionGroup`, and delegates that group plus the original state to the injected Phase 3 runtime handler; `forecasting`/`running` then goes to `forecasting_success_transition` → `END` and `forecasting`/`failed` goes to `END`; `forecasting_success_transition` applies `advance_after_forecasting`; unexpected entry, post-Phase-2, and post-forecasting phase/status combinations fail closed; no Phase-1 transition after Regulatory, no LangGraph wiring of `fail_after_forecasting`, retry/fallback execution, checkpointer, store, cache, or API wiring
- Failure-policy decisions are application-owned: `FailureAction` plus frozen `FailurePolicyContext` plus async structural `FailurePolicyPort`; concrete `InitialParallelIngestionFailurePolicy` always returns `FailureAction.FAIL`; no retry/fallback execution, no LangGraph ownership, and no graph injection
- Phase 2 failure-policy decision composition is application-owned: concrete `ParallelIngestionFailureDecisionService` injects exactly `FailurePolicyPort`. `async decide(context: FailurePolicyContext) -> FailureAction` awaits `policy.decide(context)` exactly once and returns that `FailureAction` unchanged. It does not construct context from exceptions, does not branch on the action, and does not call `fail_parallel_ingestion`. It is not wired into LangGraph. Action execution is owned by `execute_parallel_ingestion_failure_action` and composed by `ParallelIngestionFailureHandlingService`; both remain unwired.
- Phase 2 failure-policy context construction is application-owned: `build_parallel_ingestion_failure_policy_context` is a synchronous, keyword-only function that instantiates the published `FailurePolicyContext` from caller-supplied `phase`, `error_code`, `attempt_number`, and optional `agent_name`. It does not inspect exceptions, does not invent an agent identity, does not accept `WorkflowState`, and does not decide or execute a `FailureAction`. Existing constructor validation is preserved. It is not wired into LangGraph. Application-level ExceptionGroup preparation into that context is owned by `ParallelIngestionFailureContextPreparationService`; LangGraph captures groups only by delegating to the runtime handling service.
- Phase 2 failure-action execution is application-owned: `execute_parallel_ingestion_failure_action(*, state, action)` is a synchronous, keyword-only function that receives an already-decided `FailureAction`. `FAIL` returns `fail_parallel_ingestion(state)` without duplicating transition policy. `RETRY` and `FALLBACK` fail closed as `InvalidRequestError` with stable sanitized messages. The function does not construct `FailurePolicyContext`, does not call `FailurePolicyPort` or `ParallelIngestionFailureDecisionService`, does not inspect exceptions or diagnostics, and is not wired into LangGraph. Retry/fallback mechanics remain deferred.
- Phase 2 prepared failure handling is application-owned: concrete `ParallelIngestionFailureHandlingService` injects exactly `ParallelIngestionFailureDecisionService`. `async handle(*, state: WorkflowState, context: FailurePolicyContext) -> WorkflowState` awaits `decide(context)` exactly once and forwards the returned action plus the supplied state to `execute_parallel_ingestion_failure_action`. The service does not construct context, does not branch on `FailureAction`, does not call `fail_parallel_ingestion` directly, and is not imported by LangGraph. Retry/fallback execution remain deferred.
- Phase 2 agent-failure attribution is application-owned: `ParallelIngestionAgentFailure` stores canonical `AgentName` only. `ConcurrentParallelIngestionExecutor` wraps ordinary agent `Exception` failures with `raise ... from` and does not wrap cancellation. TaskGroup aggregation still propagates. Attempt tracking, multi-failure selection, and Chunk 46–49 policy/handling invocation remain deferred and unwired. One-leaf error-code classification is owned by `classify_parallel_ingestion_agent_failure` and is not invoked here.
- Phase 2 ExceptionGroup attributed-leaf extraction is application-owned: `extract_parallel_ingestion_agent_failures(failure)` recursively traverses a possibly nested `BaseExceptionGroup` and returns exact original `ParallelIngestionAgentFailure` objects in depth-first left-to-right encounter order. Duplicate agent identities are retained. Unattributed leaves fail closed as `InvalidRequestError`. The helper does not classify failures, select a primary failure, inspect `__cause__`, rebuild groups, or invoke policy. It is not wired into LangGraph or the executor.
- Phase 2 sanitized one-leaf failure classification is application-owned: `classify_parallel_ingestion_agent_failure(failure)` converts exactly one `ParallelIngestionAgentFailure` into frozen `ParallelIngestionFailureFact` with canonical `AgentName` and a stable `error_code`. `ApplicationError` causes reuse their published application error code. Non-application and missing causes collapse to `parallel_ingestion_unexpected_failure`. Exception text, class names, and tracebacks never become policy facts. The classifier does not select among multiple failures, assign attempt numbers, construct `FailurePolicyContext`, or invoke policy. It is not wired into LangGraph.
- Phase 2 tuple-level failure-fact classification is application-owned: `classify_parallel_ingestion_agent_failures(failures)` converts `tuple[ParallelIngestionAgentFailure, ...]` into `tuple[ParallelIngestionFailureFact, ...]` by delegating each already-extracted leaf to `classify_parallel_ingestion_agent_failure`. Encounter order, cardinality, and duplicate agent identities are preserved. An empty input returns an empty output. The composer does not inspect `__cause__`, does not duplicate error-code mapping, does not sort/deduplicate/group, does not select a primary failure, does not assign attempt numbers, and does not construct `FailurePolicyContext`. It is not wired into LangGraph.
- Phase 2 failure-fact selection is application-owned: non-generic `ParallelIngestionFailureSelectionPort` exposes only synchronous `select(facts: tuple[ParallelIngestionFailureFact, ...]) -> ParallelIngestionFailureFact`. Implementations satisfy the Protocol structurally. There is no first/last/agent/error/retryability/severity winner rule and no empty-tuple production default. The port does not inspect exceptions, does not assign attempt numbers, and does not construct `FailurePolicyContext`. It is not wired into LangGraph.
- Phase 2 strict single-failure selection is application-owned: concrete `StrictSingleParallelIngestionFailureSelector` structurally satisfies that Protocol without inheriting it. Exactly one sanitized fact is returned as the same instance. Zero facts and two-or-more facts fail closed as sanitized `InvalidRequestError`. Duplicate agent identities and value-equal facts are still multiple facts. There is no ranking, deduplication, aggregation, or priority table. Simultaneous multi-failure resolution remains unresolved. The selector is not wired into LangGraph.
- Phase 2 attempt-number sourcing is application-owned: non-generic `ParallelIngestionAttemptNumberPort` exposes only async `get_attempt_number(workflow_id: str) -> int`. Implementations satisfy the Protocol structurally. The returned value is the current 1-based parallel-ingestion execution attempt. The port itself has no increment/reset/set/record write API. The port does not accept `WorkflowState`, failure facts, or policy types. It is not wired into LangGraph.
- Phase 2 initial attempt-number resolution is application-owned: concrete `InitialParallelIngestionAttemptNumberSource` structurally satisfies that Protocol without inheriting it. The method remains async. The source is stateless and returns exactly `1` for every workflow identity because the published runtime has no retry execution. Workflow ID content is not inspected, hashed, validated, or persisted. There is no increment, reset, per-workflow map, or storage. Future retry support requires separately reviewed tracking semantics and likely a different concrete implementation. The source is not wired into LangGraph.
- Phase 2 failure-policy context resolution is application-owned: concrete `ParallelIngestionFailureContextResolutionService` injects exactly `ParallelIngestionFailureSelectionPort` and `ParallelIngestionAttemptNumberPort`. Keyword-only `async resolve(*, workflow_id: str, phase: WorkflowPhase, facts: tuple[ParallelIngestionFailureFact, ...]) -> FailurePolicyContext` selects once, awaits attempt lookup once, and delegates to `build_parallel_ingestion_failure_policy_context` with the supplied phase, selected error code and agent name, and resolved attempt number. The service implements no selection algorithm, no attempt tracking, no policy decision, and no action execution. Direct `FailurePolicyContext` construction is not duplicated here. The strict selector and initial attempt source exist and can be injected, but are not auto-wired. It is not wired into LangGraph.
- Phase 2 failure-context preparation is application-owned: concrete `ParallelIngestionFailureContextPreparationService` injects exactly `ParallelIngestionFailureContextResolutionService`. Keyword-only `async prepare(*, workflow_id: str, phase: WorkflowPhase, failure_group: BaseExceptionGroup) -> FailurePolicyContext` extracts attributed leaves, classifies the resulting tuple, and awaits context resolution. The service does not traverse groups itself, does not inspect causes, does not select among facts, does not resolve attempt numbers, does not invoke policy, and does not execute an action. Direct `FailurePolicyContext` construction is not duplicated here. LangGraph does not import or call it; the injected runtime failure handler owns that invocation. One attributed failure is fully preparable into context at application level; multiple attributed failures still fail closed.
- Phase 2 initial failure-policy decision is application-owned: concrete `InitialParallelIngestionFailurePolicy` structurally satisfies `FailurePolicyPort` without inheriting it. Async `decide(self, context: FailurePolicyContext) -> FailureAction` returns exactly `FailureAction.FAIL` for every valid context. The policy is stateless and does not inspect `phase`, `error_code`, `attempt_number`, or `agent_name`. It does not execute the returned action. It is intentionally conservative because retry and fallback execution do not exist. It is not auto-wired into LangGraph. Future richer policy requires separate review.
- Phase 2 runtime failure handling is application-owned: concrete `ParallelIngestionFailureRuntimeHandlingService` injects exactly `ParallelIngestionFailureContextPreparationService` and `ParallelIngestionFailureHandlingService`. Keyword-only `async handle(*, state: WorkflowState, failure_group: BaseExceptionGroup) -> WorkflowState` prepares context from `state.workflow_id`, `state.phase`, and the supplied group, then forwards that context plus the original state to existing handling. The service does not inspect exception groups, classify error codes, select facts, resolve attempt numbers, call `FailurePolicyPort`, branch on `FailureAction`, or construct a failed `WorkflowState`. LangGraph injects this service and catches only `BaseExceptionGroup` at the Phase 2 step boundary. One attributed failure terminates at `ingestion`/`failed`. Multiple attributed failures still fail closed. Direct non-group exceptions still propagate.

- Phase 3 forecasting execution is application-owned: non-generic `ForecastingExecutionPort` exposes only keyword-only `async execute(*, plan: ForecastingPlan) -> ForecastingSuccess`. Empty tuples remain valid successful outputs. Native TaskGroup cancellation and exception aggregation apply on branch failure. Ordinary Consumer Load and DAM `Exception` values are wrapped as `ForecastingAgentFailure` with canonical `AgentName` and `raise ... from` chaining; sibling cancellation is not wrapped. Chunk 189 adds a separate API-owned `build_forecasting_workflow_step` that only constructs `ForecastingWorkflowStep` from a caller-supplied context port and a caller-supplied `ForecastingExecutionPort`; it does not call the Chunk 188 builder or construct this executor.
