- Phase 3 forecasting workflow context is application-owned: non-generic `ForecastingWorkflowContextPort` exposes only keyword-only `async resolve_plan(*, state: WorkflowState) -> ForecastingPlan` and `async record_success(*, state: WorkflowState, success: ForecastingSuccess) -> None`. Implementations satisfy the Protocol structurally. Plan and success stay outside the seven-field `WorkflowState` snapshot. The port does not choose storage technology, execution strategy, retry, fallback, or degraded semantics. Infrastructure `InMemoryForecastingWorkflowContext` is a process-local structural implementation for local/dev use; it is not durable storage, not Redis/PostgreSQL, not wired into LangGraph/`create_app()`, and does not choose forecast execution order. There is no generic `WorkflowContextPort`, and LangGraph does not import this port or the in-memory adapter.
- Parallel Phase 2 successful fan-in is application-owned: frozen `ParallelIngestionSuccess` composes the five existing typed result DTOs for the all-five-success case only; it is not `WorkflowState`, does not execute agents, does not use LangGraph, and does not encode partial/failure/degraded semantics
- Parallel Phase 2 execution is application-owned: non-generic `ParallelIngestionExecutionPort` exposes only `async execute(plan: ParallelIngestionPlan) -> ParallelIngestionSuccess`. Concrete `ConcurrentParallelIngestionExecutor` injects the five application agents and uses `asyncio.TaskGroup` for the all-success path only. Ordinary agent failures are wrapped as `ParallelIngestionAgentFailure` with canonical `AgentName` and `raise ... from` chaining. Sibling cancellation is not wrapped. Native TaskGroup failure propagation still applies. There is no retry/fallback/degraded policy, no error-code classification inside the executor, no LangGraph wiring, and no `WorkflowState` expansion
- Parallel Phase 2 workflow context is application-owned: non-generic `ParallelIngestionWorkflowContextPort` exposes only `async resolve_plan(workflow_id) -> ParallelIngestionPlan` and `async record_success(workflow_id, success) -> None`. `workflow_id` reuses the published `WorkflowState.workflow_id` type. Plan and success stay outside the seven-field `WorkflowState` snapshot. Infrastructure `InMemoryParallelIngestionWorkflowContext` is a process-local structural implementation for local/dev use; it is not durable storage, not Redis/PostgreSQL, not wired into LangGraph/`create_app()`, and does not encode failure/degraded semantics. Production/durable workflow-context implementation remains absent.
- Regulatory Intelligence workflow context is application-owned: non-generic `RegulatoryIntelligenceWorkflowContextPort` exposes only keyword-only `async resolve_request(*, state: WorkflowState) -> RegulatoryIntelligenceWorkflowRequest` and `async record_result(*, state: WorkflowState, result: RegulatoryIntelligenceResult) -> None`. The published seven-field `WorkflowState` is reused as identity; Chunk 114 request and existing Regulatory result stay outside that snapshot. There is no production implementation, no Redis/PostgreSQL adapter, no generic `WorkflowContextPort`, and no LangGraph wiring.
- Regulatory Intelligence workflow-node coordination is application-owned: concrete `RegulatoryIntelligenceWorkflowNodeAdapter` injects exactly `RegulatoryIntelligenceWorkflowContextPort` and `RegulatoryIntelligenceWorkflowStep`. `async run(state: WorkflowState) -> WorkflowState` is resolve → step `run` → record, then return of the original state object. There is no phase/status mutation, no LangGraph import inside the adapter, and no retry/fallback. LangGraph injects the already-constructed adapter into node `regulatory_intelligence` on contract/running and then ends. Regulatory request/result remain outside `WorkflowState`. There is no Pricing & Sales node and no Phase-1 transition.
- Parallel Phase 2 workflow composition is application-owned: concrete `ParallelIngestionWorkflowStep` injects exactly `ParallelIngestionWorkflowContextPort` and `ParallelIngestionExecutionPort`. `async run(state: WorkflowState) -> WorkflowState` is resolve → execute → record, then return of the original state object. There is no phase/status mutation, no `FailurePolicyPort`, and no retry/fallback/degraded policy. LangGraph injects the step into `parallel_ingestion`; it does not reconstruct the sequence inside the node
- UTC timestamps; MW vs MWh; Decimal money; explicit currency codes
- Application/domain exceptions contain no HTTP semantics; HTTP translation is API-only
- Unexpected exception details are never sent to clients
- Correlation IDs propagate through logs and error responses
- ML ≠ LLM for numerical forecasts; agents never import concrete ML implementations
- No secrets in git; `.env` must not be committed
- Avoid always-on heavy local services

## Current services

TimescaleDB is available on demand under the Compose `postgres` profile (`timescale/timescaledb:2.29.2-pg17`, loopback-only). Redis is available on demand under the Compose `redis` profile (`redis:8.2.9-alpine`, loopback-only, password required, non-persistent). Qdrant is available on demand under the Compose `qdrant` profile (`qdrant/qdrant:v1.19.1`, loopback-only REST 6333, API key required, telemetry disabled, named `qdrant-data`). n8n is available on demand under the Compose `n8n` profile (`n8nio/n8n:2.37.10`, loopback-only HTTP 5678, encryption key required, telemetry/template/version/personalization disabled, named `n8n-data`). None is assumed to be running continuously. The local Qdrant and n8n profiles are not production-ready. The n8n container does not implement ingestion.

## Current APIs

- `GET /api/v1/health` — process liveness only (`API_CONTRACTS.md`)
- `POST /api/v1/regulatory-intelligence/query` — production `create_app()` installs the published Regulatory query router under the existing API prefix; execution still depends on the lifespan-scoped service; construction remains lazy; offline/test lifespans can still avoid provider credentials
- Standard error envelope and `X-Correlation-ID` on API responses, including health and unknown routes

## Current agents

Ten concrete application agents exist:

1. Weather & Renewable Forecast Agent (`WeatherAndRenewableForecastAgent`). It is provider-neutral and unwired: it consumes `WeatherAndRenewableForecastRequest`, calls `WeatherRecordSourcePort`, and returns `WeatherAndRenewableForecastResult` wrapping canonical `WeatherRecord` values. No weather provider, ACL adapter, persistence, or graph/API wiring exists.
2. Hydro Resources Agent (`HydroResourcesAgent`). It is provider-neutral and unwired: it consumes `HydroResourcesRequest`, calls `HydroRecordSourcePort`, and returns `HydroResourcesResult` wrapping canonical `HydroRecord` values. It does not calculate reservoir behavior or available generation. No hydro provider, ACL adapter, persistence, graph/API wiring, or Generation Availability coupling exists.
3. Generation Availability Agent (`GenerationAvailabilityAgent`). It is provider-neutral and unwired: it consumes `GenerationAvailabilityRequest`, calls `GenerationAvailabilityRecordSourcePort`, and returns `GenerationAvailabilityResult` wrapping canonical `GenerationAvailabilityRecord` values. It does not infer status, calculate capacity, assume missing-asset completeness, or derive records from Hydro. No generation provider, ACL adapter, persistence, graph/API wiring, or Hydro coupling exists.
4. News Intelligence Agent (`NewsIntelligenceAgent`). It is provider-neutral and unwired: it consumes `NewsIntelligenceRequest`, calls `NewsEventSourcePort`, and returns `NewsIntelligenceResult` wrapping canonical `NewsEvent` values. It does not scrape, summarize, classify, infer sentiment/relevance/impact, or call an LLM. No news provider, ACL adapter, persistence, graph/API wiring, or embedding/Qdrant indexing exists.
5. Market Monitoring Agent (`MarketMonitoringAgent`). It is provider-neutral and unwired: it consumes `MarketMonitoringRequest`, calls `MarketPriceRecordSourcePort`, and returns `MarketMonitoringResult` wrapping canonical `MarketPriceRecord` values. It does not convert currency, assume cadence or interval duration, fabricate missing prices, clear the market, or forecast prices. No market provider, ACL adapter, persistence, graph/API wiring, or market-status contract exists.
6. Regulatory Intelligence Agent (`RegulatoryIntelligenceAgent`). It is provider-neutral and unwired from graph and `create_app()`: it consumes `RegulatoryIntelligenceRequest`, calls existing `DocumentVectorSearchPort` with the supplied `DocumentVectorSearchQuery`, and — when retrieved chunks exist — calls `RegulatoryConstraintInferencePort`. Empty retrieval returns `constraints=()` and does not invoke inference. The agent does not construct `RegulatoryConstraint` values, hardcode Armenian DAM rules, embed query text, or call an LLM SDK. Query-text embedding exists as a separate `DocumentQueryEmbeddingPort`. Query preparation exists as a separate `DocumentVectorSearchQueryPreparationService`. Application query execution composition exists as `RegulatoryIntelligenceQueryExecutionService`. An application-owned workflow step (`RegulatoryIntelligenceWorkflowStep`) structurally satisfies existing `AgentPort[RegulatoryIntelligenceWorkflowRequest, RegulatoryIntelligenceResult]` over that service and remains unwired from LangGraph. An application-owned workflow-context Protocol (`RegulatoryIntelligenceWorkflowContextPort`) resolves a typed Chunk 114 request from `WorkflowState` and records the existing `RegulatoryIntelligenceResult` without embedding those values on the snapshot; there is no production context implementation. An application-owned workflow-node adapter (`RegulatoryIntelligenceWorkflowNodeAdapter`) coordinates that context with the Chunk 114 step as resolve → step `run` → record and returns the original `WorkflowState` unchanged; LangGraph injects that already-constructed adapter on contract/running and terminates at END. Outer `build_regulatory_intelligence_query_execution` constructs that stack from the three injected application ports without provider construction or I/O. Provider-aware `build_regulatory_intelligence_provider_runtime` constructs the published OpenAI query-embedding adapter, Qdrant search adapter, and OpenAI inference adapter from injected clients, existing `QdrantDocumentVectorConfig`, and two explicit model strings, then delegates to that builder. Configured `build_regulatory_intelligence_configured_runtime` maps already-constructed `RegulatoryIntelligenceRuntimeSettings` into `QdrantDocumentVectorConfig` plus the two model strings and delegates to that provider-aware builder. Managed `managed_regulatory_intelligence_runtime` accepts already-loaded typed settings including `QdrantDocumentVectorDistanceSettings`, constructs clients through existing factories, awaits Chunk 112 verification on that same managed Qdrant client, then delegates to that configured builder, yields the query-execution service, and closes both clients. Settings-loaded `loaded_regulatory_intelligence_runtime` loads those four typed settings through the existing loaders, including `load_qdrant_document_vector_distance_settings`, and delegates to that managed runtime. FastAPI-compatible `build_regulatory_intelligence_lifespan` enters and exits that loaded runtime and exposes the exact service on `app.state` while active; production `create_app()` installs that callback by default. A dedicated HTTP query router uses `Depends(get_regulatory_intelligence_query_execution_service)` to invoke the query-execution service and map canonical constraints to the existing HTTP DTO; production `create_app()` installs that router under the existing API prefix. This agent still expects an already-built `DocumentVectorSearchQuery` and does not import OpenAI or Qdrant. An offline typed OpenAI client-construction foundation exists (`OpenAISettings`, `create_openai_client`) and is used by the Chunk 74 managed runtime; `load_openai_settings` is used by the Chunk 75 loaded runtime; neither is used by this agent, either adapter, the Chunk 67/71/73 builders, `create_app()`, or LangGraph. Typed `RegulatoryIntelligenceRuntimeSettings` exists for the two model identifiers plus Regulatory Qdrant collection name and vector size; the Chunk 73 builder and Chunk 74 managed runtime receive an already-constructed instance and do not load it; Chunk 75 loads it through the published loader; it is not used by this agent, either adapter, the Chunk 67/71 builders, `create_app()`, or LangGraph. No PDF/OCR, actual RAG runtime, Pricing & Sales, post-Regulatory Phase-1 continuation, production/API graph composition, or verified Armenian rule extraction exists.
7. Consumer Load Forecast Agent (`ConsumerLoadForecastAgent`). It is application-owned and unwired: it consumes the published `ConsumerLoadForecastModelRequest`, awaits `ConsumerLoadForecastModelPort.forecast` exactly once, and returns that `tuple[LoadForecastPoint, ...]` unchanged. It does not calculate forecasts, reconstruct points, convert MW, or call an LLM. The published request DTO can be composed into unwired `ForecastingPlan`. The returned canonical tuple can be composed into unwired `ForecastingSuccess`. Application-owned `ForecastingExecutionPort` can accept that plan and return that success aggregate; concrete `ParallelForecastingExecutionService` implements that port concurrently and remains unwired from LangGraph/`create_app()`. Application-owned `ForecastingWorkflowContextPort` can resolve that plan from `WorkflowState` and record that success outside the snapshot; infrastructure `InMemoryForecastingWorkflowContext` is an unwired local/dev structural implementation and is not production persistence. Application-owned `ForecastingWorkflowStep` can compose resolve → execute → record over those ports and return the original `WorkflowState` unchanged; concurrent Consumer Load and DAM execution is owned by `ParallelForecastingExecutionService`. LangGraph injects that already-composed step on `forecasting`/`running` and then applies `forecasting_success_transition`. Application-owned `advance_after_forecasting` maps a valid `forecasting`/`running` snapshot to `risk_and_bid`/`running` without forecast payloads; LangGraph node `forecasting_success_transition` delegates to that function. Application-owned `fail_after_forecasting` can map `forecasting`/`running` to `forecasting`/`failed` without mutating diagnostics and remains unwired from LangGraph, the executor, and the workflow step. An unwired ML-owned previous-day persistence baseline now structurally implements the model port; the agent is not production-wired to that model. ML also owns an unwired previous-day persistence backtest-case builder; the agent does not execute backtests. ML also owns an unwired previous-day persistence MAE evaluator over already-built cases; the agent does not perform evaluation. ML also owns an unwired exact 24h lag feature-row builder; the agent does not prepare features. ML also owns an unwired chronological feature-row split at an explicit UTC cutoff; the agent does not split rows. ML also owns an unwired one-feature OLS parameter fit; the agent does not fit or use those parameters. ML also owns an unwired lag-24h OLS evaluation prediction over already-fitted parameters and already-built evaluation rows; the agent does not predict from that fit. ML also owns an unwired lag-24h OLS MAE evaluator over already-produced prediction artifacts; the agent does not score those predictions. ML also owns an unwired aligned persistence-versus-trained OLS MAE comparison over already-produced persistence cases and trained predictions; the agent does not compare those artifacts. ML also owns an unwired exact 24h plus 168h lag feature-row builder; the agent does not prepare those richer rows. ML also owns an unwired chronological split of those already-built two-lag rows at an explicit UTC cutoff; the agent does not split those rows. ML also owns an unwired two-feature OLS parameter fit over those already-built training rows; the agent does not fit or use those parameters. ML also owns an unwired two-feature OLS evaluation prediction over already-fitted parameters and already-built evaluation rows; the agent does not predict from that fit. ML also owns an unwired two-feature OLS MAE evaluator over already-produced prediction artifacts; the agent does not score those predictions. ML also owns an unwired aligned one-feature-versus-two-feature OLS MAE comparison over already-produced one-feature and two-feature predictions; the agent does not compare those artifacts. ML also owns an unwired aligned persistence-versus-two-feature OLS MAE comparison over already-produced persistence cases and two-feature predictions; the agent does not compare those artifacts. There is no production-wired trained ML adapter, wider feature pipeline, RMSE/MSE/MAPE, or API/runtime composition of this agent. An unwired two-feature OLS candidate live adapter (`Lag24h168hOLSConsumerLoadForecastModel`) now exists and is not a selected production model.
8. DAM Price Forecast Agent (`DAMPriceForecastAgent`). It is application-owned and unwired: it consumes the published `DAMPriceForecastModelRequest` (which now carries caller-supplied `forecast_run_id` and `generated_at` identity from Chunk 169 alongside explicit market, currency, canonical history, and explicit target timestamps), awaits `DAMPriceForecastModelPort.forecast` exactly once, and returns that `tuple[PriceForecastPoint, ...]` unchanged. It does not calculate prices, reconstruct points, convert currencies, or call an LLM. The published request DTO can be composed into unwired `ForecastingPlan`. The returned canonical tuple can be composed into unwired `ForecastingSuccess`. Application-owned `ForecastingExecutionPort` can accept that plan and return that success aggregate; concrete `ParallelForecastingExecutionService` implements that port concurrently and remains unwired from LangGraph/`create_app()`. Application-owned `ForecastingWorkflowContextPort` can resolve that plan from `WorkflowState` and record that success outside the snapshot; infrastructure `InMemoryForecastingWorkflowContext` is an unwired local/dev structural implementation and is not production persistence. Application-owned `ForecastingWorkflowStep` can compose resolve → execute → record over those ports and return the original `WorkflowState` unchanged; concurrent Consumer Load and DAM execution is owned by `ParallelForecastingExecutionService`. LangGraph injects that already-composed step on `forecasting`/`running` and then applies `forecasting_success_transition`. Application-owned `advance_after_forecasting` maps a valid `forecasting`/`running` snapshot to `risk_and_bid`/`running` without forecast payloads; LangGraph node `forecasting_success_transition` delegates to that function. Application-owned `fail_after_forecasting` can map `forecasting`/`running` to `forecasting`/`failed` without mutating diagnostics and remains unwired from LangGraph, the executor, and the workflow step. There is no production ML adapter wired to this agent, no feature pipeline, and no API/runtime composition. Chunk 170 adds one ML-owned, completely unwired reference baseline: `PreviousDayPersistenceDAMPriceForecastModel` in `energy_trading.ml.dam_price` structurally implements `DAMPriceForecastModelPort` by copying the canonical `EnergyPrice` from the unique history observation at exactly elapsed ``T - 24h``; it passes `forecast_run_id`, `generated_at`, and `market_id` through from the request, preserves negative canonical prices unchanged, converts no currency, uses no nearest/interpolation/weekly/session fallback, and fails closed with `InvalidRequestError` on missing or ambiguous exact lag. The DAM agent does not import or construct it, no runtime composition injects it, and no production model has been selected. Chunk 187 adds a second structural ML candidate, `Lag24h168hOLSDAMPriceForecastModel` (exact `T - 24h` / `T - 168h` Decimal OLS live candidate), alongside `PreviousDayPersistenceDAMPriceForecastModel`; the agent is wired to neither concrete implementation, imports and constructs neither, and no production-selected DAM adapter exists.
9. Portfolio & Risk Agent (`PortfolioRiskAgent`, Chunk 198, published at `9df9913d2b4ec4e3eeaaf70dc9dc4561d9c12e11`, tag `chunk-198`). Chunk 201 (published at `33a143c54265d7b4535ea1f948d8bb05fd33be51`, tag `chunk-201`) adds API-owned, unwired `build_portfolio_risk_execution_service`, which constructs this unchanged agent from a caller-supplied assessment port and wraps it in `PortfolioRiskExecutionService` with a caller-supplied forecast reader; it selects no assessment implementation and does not make the agent a running production participant. Chunk 205 (published at `0941bec11e01259bcd828775631e22a4b758ed05`, tag `chunk-205`, test-only) verifies offline that this real agent, built by that real builder over the same read-after-write wrapper the compiled forecasting graph wrote to, returns a deterministic test-only assessment that the test then hands to the real Trading Strategy execution service; the assessment is fixture data, not risk approval. Chunk 200 (published at `25efd7688e25ee8690a9e734f504be7d0af332fb`, tag `chunk-200`, test-only) verifies offline that a forecasting success recorded by the real compiled graph reaches this agent through `PortfolioRiskExecutionService` over the same read-after-write wrapper; the final assessment comes from a deterministic test-only assessment port. Chunk 199 (published at `8fb8bf163f8789b7268e1aa00ddff0fb9089aa28`, tag `chunk-199`) adds a separate application use case, `PortfolioRiskExecutionService`, that reads `ForecastingSuccess` by exact workflow identity, assembles the request from caller-supplied inputs, and invokes this agent once through `AgentPort`; the agent itself is unchanged. It is application-owned and unwired: it consumes the published Chunk 197 `PortfolioRiskAssessmentRequest`, awaits the keyword-only injected `PortfolioRiskAssessmentPort.assess(request=request)` exactly once, and returns that exact canonical `RiskAssessment` unchanged. It is a thin delegate only, not a functioning risk-calculation system: it does not calculate exposure, VaR, scenarios, or thresholds, infer completeness, default a risk result, read forecasts, inspect `WorkflowState`, retry, fall back, or call an LLM. No concrete assessment implementation, Phase 4 graph wiring, or `create_app()` wiring exists.
10. Trading Strategy Agent (`TradingStrategyAgent`, Chunk 203, published at `ff23382d95193f9cabc8f92649b3bef07f46d5cb`, tag `chunk-203`). It is application-owned and unwired: it consumes the published `TradingStrategyBidRequest`, awaits `TradingStrategyBidPort.propose_bids` exactly once, and returns that `tuple[MarketBid, ...]` unchanged; no bid-port implementation exists. Chunk 205 (published at `0941bec11e01259bcd828775631e22a4b758ed05`, tag `chunk-205`, test-only) verifies offline that this real agent, behind the real `TradingStrategyExecutionService`, receives the exact compiled-graph `ForecastingSuccess` and the exact Portfolio Risk `RiskAssessment` and returns a deterministic test-only bid tuple unchanged; the bids are fixture data, not authorization to submit, and no production orchestration invokes it. Chunk 204 (published at `4d9a2540d728d45057adbd26b364aa151d1ed8bf`, tag `chunk-204`) adds application-owned, unwired `TradingStrategyExecutionService`, which assembles that request from explicit caller-supplied canonical inputs and invokes this unchanged agent through the generic `AgentPort`; it is a service, not an additional agent, and it does not approve, price, submit, or persist bids. Chunk 206 (published at `d6b98148f51322f43cfaf655d0598ee00950afd5`, tag `chunk-206`) adds API-owned, unwired `build_trading_strategy_execution_service`, which constructs this agent once from one caller-supplied `TradingStrategyBidPort` and that execution service once over it; it selects no bid-port implementation, executes nothing, and does not make this agent a running production participant. Chunk 207 (published at `d04fdf4dbc14ad5b9072100dd2837bc46a1eda13`, tag `chunk-207`) adds application-owned, unwired `RiskAndBidProposalHandoff`, an immutable data record that can carry this agent's proposal tuple beside the Portfolio & Risk `RiskAssessment` and the forecasting result; it does not invoke this agent, is not an agent, and implies no approval, eligibility, or submission. Chunk 208 (published at `8c71a22f0e1513c8981583170c2ce9026cbeecd8`, tag `chunk-208`) adds application-owned, unwired `TradingStrategyProposalHandoffService`, which awaits the published `TradingStrategyExecutionService` once (and therefore this agent once) with caller-supplied forecasting and risk results and returns one `RiskAndBidProposalHandoff` carrying the exact proposals; it is a service, not an agent or a Risk & Bid coordinator. Chunk 209 (published at `926c0dd2d2632433cc94b4db8076a8d5ca04cda3`, tag `chunk-209`) adds API-owned, unwired, construction-only `build_trading_strategy_proposal_handoff_service`, which calls the published Chunk 206 builder once (constructing this agent once) and wraps the resulting execution service in one `TradingStrategyProposalHandoffService`; it executes nothing, is not an agent, and does not make this agent a running production participant. Chunk 210 (local/unpublished, test-only) verifies offline that this real agent, built by that real builder and invoked by test code after the real Portfolio Risk stack, receives the exact compiled-graph `ForecastingSuccess` and the exact fixture `RiskAssessment` and that its deterministic test-only bid tuple is carried unchanged into the published `RiskAndBidProposalHandoff`; it adds no agent and no production invocation path. The concrete-agent count remains 10.

LangGraph does not call these agents or `AgentPort` directly. It injects `RegulatoryIntelligenceWorkflowNodeAdapter` into node `regulatory_intelligence` and awaits `run(state)` on contract/running, then ends without a Phase-1 transition. It injects `ParallelIngestionWorkflowStep` into node `parallel_ingestion` and awaits `run(state)`. That step can invoke the five Phase 2 agents only when a caller has composed `ParallelIngestionExecutionPort` (typically `ConcurrentParallelIngestionExecutor`) and a context port behind it. It injects `ForecastingWorkflowStep` into node `forecasting` and awaits `run(state)` on forecasting/running, then node `forecasting_success_transition` applies `advance_after_forecasting` so a successful Phase-3 graph run ends at `RISK_AND_BID`/`RUNNING`. `RegulatoryIntelligenceWorkflowContextPort` is Protocol-only and is not imported by LangGraph. `RegulatoryIntelligenceWorkflowNodeAdapter` remains framework-neutral; the graph invokes that already-constructed adapter and does not reconstruct resolve → step → record. After a successful Phase 2 step, node `parallel_ingestion_success_transition` applies `advance_after_parallel_ingestion`, so a valid `ingestion`/`running` graph run ends at `forecasting`/`running` without entering the forecasting node. If the step raises `BaseExceptionGroup`, the graph delegates that exact group and the original state to the injected `ParallelIngestionFailureRuntimeHandlingService`; the supported one-attributed-failure path ends at `ingestion`/`failed` without the success transition. Direct non-group exceptions still propagate. Application-owned `fail_parallel_ingestion` can replace a valid `ingestion`/`running` snapshot with `ingestion`/`failed` without changing diagnostics; the graph does not call it directly. Application-owned `ParallelIngestionFailureDecisionService` can decide a `FailureAction` from an already-constructed `FailurePolicyContext` without executing it; the graph does not call it. Application-owned `build_parallel_ingestion_failure_policy_context` can construct that published context from already-sanitized typed facts; the graph does not call it. Application-owned `execute_parallel_ingestion_failure_action` can apply terminal `FailureAction.FAIL` by delegating to `fail_parallel_ingestion` and rejects `RETRY` / `FALLBACK` as not implemented; the graph does not call it. Application-owned `ParallelIngestionFailureHandlingService` can compose an already-built `FailurePolicyContext` through the published decision service and action executor; the graph does not call it. Application-owned `ConcurrentParallelIngestionExecutor` attributes ordinary Phase 2 agent failures as `ParallelIngestionAgentFailure` with canonical `AgentName` before TaskGroup aggregation; the graph does not interpret those leaves. Application-owned `extract_parallel_ingestion_agent_failures` can extract those attributed leaves from a possibly nested exception group without classifying them; the graph does not call it. Application-owned `classify_parallel_ingestion_agent_failure` can convert one attributed leaf into sanitized `ParallelIngestionFailureFact`; the graph does not call it. Application-owned `classify_parallel_ingestion_agent_failures` can classify an already-extracted attributed-failure tuple by delegating each element to that one-leaf classifier; it preserves order/cardinality/duplicates, does not select a primary failure, and the graph does not call it. Application-owned `ParallelIngestionFailureSelectionPort` can accept that sanitized fact tuple and return one `ParallelIngestionFailureFact`; there is no first/last/priority winner rule, and the graph does not call it. Application-owned `StrictSingleParallelIngestionFailureSelector` implements that contract for the unambiguous one-fact case only; zero or multiple facts fail closed, simultaneous multi-failure selection remains unresolved, and the graph does not call it. Application-owned `ParallelIngestionAttemptNumberPort` can return the current 1-based attempt for a workflow identity; there is no increment/reset API on the port, and the graph does not call it. Application-owned `InitialParallelIngestionAttemptNumberSource` implements that contract for the current no-retry runtime by returning exactly `1`; it is not a tracker, and the graph does not call it. Application-owned `ParallelIngestionFailureContextResolutionService` can compose a sanitized fact tuple, that selection port, that attempt-number port, and the published context builder into one `FailurePolicyContext`; the strict selector and initial attempt source exist but are not auto-wired, and the graph does not call it. Application-owned `ParallelIngestionFailureContextPreparationService` can compose a `BaseExceptionGroup` through extraction, tuple classification, and that resolution service into one `FailurePolicyContext`; it does not invoke policy or execute an action, and the graph does not call it. Application-owned `InitialParallelIngestionFailurePolicy` can decide `FailureAction.FAIL` for every valid prepared context; it does not execute the action, and the graph does not call it. Application-owned `ParallelIngestionFailureRuntimeHandlingService` can compose a `WorkflowState` plus `BaseExceptionGroup` through preparation then existing handling into terminal `ingestion`/`failed` on the supported single-failure path; LangGraph injects it at the Phase 2 step boundary and does not reconstruct that pipeline. `InMemoryParallelIngestionWorkflowContext` exists as unwired local/dev infrastructure. Infrastructure `InMemoryForecastingWorkflowContext` exists as unwired local/dev infrastructure for Phase 3 plan/success payloads. There is no production/durable workflow-context implementation, no API/composition wiring, and no retry/fallback execution. `ParallelIngestionWorkflowContextPort` can resolve a plan and record all-five-success output by workflow identity without embedding Phase 2 payloads in `WorkflowState`. Failure/degraded fan-in policy does not exist. Failure handling is not complete. None of these agents constitutes complete production ingestion. Regulatory RAG is not operational.

The other 3 concrete agents remain absent. Consumer Load Forecast now has an application-owned agent (`ConsumerLoadForecastAgent`) that structurally satisfies `AgentPort[ConsumerLoadForecastModelRequest, tuple[LoadForecastPoint, ...]]` by injecting `ConsumerLoadForecastModelPort` and awaiting `forecast(request=request)` exactly once. The published request carries required caller-supplied `forecast_run_id` and `generated_at`, identifies the target consumer with canonical `EntityId`, reuses canonical `ConsumptionRecord` history (`value_mw` load/power in MW), and existing `LoadForecastPoint` output (MW). Empty history is allowed; non-empty history must match that consumer. The request DTO does not generate run identity or generation time. An ML-owned previous-day persistence baseline (`PreviousDayPersistenceConsumerLoadForecastModel`) now structurally implements the model port as exact `T - 24h` MW copy; missing or duplicate exact lag observations fail closed; the adapter remains completely unwired from the agent, API, LangGraph, and `ForecastingExecutionPort`. ML-owned `build_previous_day_persistence_backtest_cases` now builds chronological exact `T` / `T - 24h` evaluation cases from historical `ConsumptionRecord` values; missing pairs are skipped; duplicate timestamps or mixed consumers fail closed; the builder remains unwired. ML-owned `evaluate_previous_day_persistence_mae` scores those already-built cases into `PreviousDayPersistenceMAEResult` (`case_count`, `mae_mw`); empty input fails closed as existing `InvalidRequestError`; MAE remains MW; RMSE/MSE/MAPE are not calculated; the evaluator remains unwired. ML-owned `build_consumer_load_lag_24h_feature_rows` now builds chronological exact `T` / `T - 24h` supervised feature rows (`ConsumerLoadLag24hFeatureRow`) from historical `ConsumptionRecord` values; `lag_24h_mw` is the only numerical feature and `target_value_mw` is the supervised label; missing pairs are skipped; duplicate timestamps or mixed consumers fail closed; the builder remains unwired and does not depend on Chunk 132–134 inference/backtest/evaluation artifacts. ML-owned `evaluate_consumer_load_lag_24h_168h_linear_regression_mae` now scores those already-produced prediction artifacts into `ConsumerLoadLag24h168hLinearRegressionMAEResult` (`case_count`, `mae_mw`); empty input, mixed consumers, malformed chronology, and non-finite values or MAE fail closed; negative finite predictions are scored with ordinary absolute error; the evaluator remains unwired, does not depend on Chunk 139 one-feature MAE, and does not add RMSE/MSE/MAPE or model comparison. ML-owned `compare_consumer_load_persistence_vs_lag_24h_vs_lag_24h_168h_ols_mae` now aligns already-produced Chunk 133 persistence cases with already-produced Chunk 138 one-feature predictions and already-produced Chunk 144 two-feature predictions on the same consumer, same target timestamps, same actual MW labels, and equal cohort size, then delegates to the published Chunk 134, Chunk 139, and Chunk 145 MAE evaluators and returns `ConsumerLoadPersistenceVsLag24hVsLag24h168hOLSMAEComparison` (`case_count`, `persistence_mae_mw`, `lag_24h_mae_mw`, `lag_24h_168h_mae_mw`); empty, unequal, mixed-consumer, mismatched-consumer, malformed-chronology, timestamp-mismatch, and actual-mismatch inputs fail closed; the comparison does not select a production model, does not compute a relative change, and does not call the published pairwise comparison functions; it remains unwired from agents, API, LangGraph, and `ForecastingExecutionPort`. ML-owned `Lag24h168hOLSConsumerLoadForecastModel` now structurally implements the model port as an unwired two-feature OLS candidate live adapter from already-fitted Chunk 143 parameters and exact `T - 24h` / `T - 168h` history; missing or duplicate lags fail closed; non-finite or negative finite live predictions fail closed before `LoadForecastPoint` construction; exact `0.0` is admitted; there is no clamping, persistence fallback, or production-model selection; Chunk 144 offline signed predictions remain unchanged. There is no production-wired trained ML adapter, no wider feature pipeline, and no LangGraph/API wiring of that agent or port. An unwired two-feature OLS candidate live adapter (`Lag24h168hOLSConsumerLoadForecastModel`) now exists and is not a selected production model. DAM Price Forecast now has an application-owned agent (`DAMPriceForecastAgent`) that structurally satisfies `AgentPort[DAMPriceForecastModelRequest, tuple[PriceForecastPoint, ...]]` by injecting `DAMPriceForecastModelPort` and awaiting `forecast(request=request)` exactly once. The published request identifies the target market with canonical `EntityId` and the target currency with canonical `CurrencyCode` independently of history, reuses canonical `MarketPriceRecord` history, and existing `PriceForecastPoint` output (`EnergyPrice`). Empty history is allowed; non-empty history must match that market and currency. There is no currency conversion, no numerical forecasting logic in the agent, no production ML adapter, no feature pipeline, and no LangGraph/API wiring of that agent or port. Application-owned `ForecastingPlan` can hold those two published request DTOs together without executing either agent or choosing an execution order. Application-owned `ForecastingSuccess` can hold the two canonical forecast-point tuples together without executing either agent or choosing an execution order. Application-owned `ForecastingExecutionPort` can accept that plan and return that success aggregate; concurrent Consumer Load and DAM execution is owned by unwired `ParallelForecastingExecutionService`. Application-owned `ForecastingWorkflowContextPort` can resolve that plan from `WorkflowState` and record that success without embedding Phase-3 payloads on the snapshot; infrastructure `InMemoryForecastingWorkflowContext` is an unwired local/dev structural implementation and LangGraph does not import it. Application-owned `ForecastingWorkflowStep` can compose that resolve → execute → record sequence and return the original `WorkflowState` unchanged; concurrent execution is owned by `ParallelForecastingExecutionService`, which remains unwired from LangGraph/`create_app()`. LangGraph injects that already-composed step on `forecasting`/`running` and then applies `forecasting_success_transition`. Application-owned `advance_after_forecasting` maps a valid `forecasting`/`running` snapshot to `risk_and_bid`/`running` without embedding forecast payloads; application-owned `fail_after_forecasting` maps a valid `forecasting`/`running` snapshot to `forecasting`/`failed` without mutating diagnostics and remains unwired from LangGraph. ML vendor remains unspecified. Thirteen canonical identities are captured by application `AgentName`. Future agents will structurally satisfy `AgentPort` and consume canonical contracts through application ports; they must not parse raw source schemas.

## Current ML models

Chunk 132 added ML-owned `PreviousDayPersistenceConsumerLoadForecastModel`, an unwired deterministic Consumer Load baseline that structurally implements `ConsumerLoadForecastModelPort`. For each requested target `T` it copies canonical MW from the unique history observation at exactly `T - 24 hours`; missing or duplicate exact lag observations fail closed as existing `InvalidRequestError`; caller-supplied `forecast_run_id`, `generated_at`, and `consumer_id` pass through onto `LoadForecastPoint`. It is a benchmark/reference implementation, not a trained champion model, and is not wired into agents, API composition, FastAPI, LangGraph, or `ForecastingExecutionPort`. Chunk 133 added ML-owned `PreviousDayPersistenceBacktestCase` and `build_previous_day_persistence_backtest_cases`: historical observations define candidate targets; a case exists only for an exact `T` / `T - 24h` pair; output is chronological by `target_timestamp`; missing exact lags are skipped rather than imputed; mixed-consumer or duplicate-timestamp history fails closed as existing `InvalidRequestError`; canonical MW is copied unchanged; no residual/MAE/MSE/RMSE/MAPE is calculated by the builder; the builder is not runtime inference and remains unwired from agents, API, LangGraph, and `ForecastingExecutionPort`. Chunk 134 added ML-owned `PreviousDayPersistenceMAEResult` and `evaluate_previous_day_persistence_mae`: it consumes already-built `PreviousDayPersistenceBacktestCase` values, returns `case_count` plus MW MAE as the arithmetic mean of absolute predicted-minus-actual errors, fails closed on an empty case tuple with existing `InvalidRequestError`, does not rebuild history or backtest cases, and remains unwired from agents, API, LangGraph, and `ForecastingExecutionPort`. RMSE, MSE, MAPE, residuals, generic metrics, training, and wider feature engineering are not introduced. Chunk 135 added ML-owned `ConsumerLoadLag24hFeatureRow` and `build_consumer_load_lag_24h_feature_rows`: historical observations define candidate targets; a row exists only for an exact `T` / `T - 24h` pair; `lag_24h_mw` is the model input feature copied from the lag observation; `target_value_mw` is the supervised target copied from `T`; output is chronological by `target_timestamp`; missing exact lags are skipped rather than imputed; mixed-consumer or duplicate-timestamp history fails closed as existing `InvalidRequestError`; canonical MW is copied unchanged; the builder does not depend on Chunk 132 inference, Chunk 133 backtest cases, or Chunk 134 MAE evaluation; it remains unwired from agents, API, LangGraph, and `ForecastingExecutionPort`.
